Multi-Agent Systems

Multi-Agent Systems — a source-grounded lineage account with every original property individually addressable.

AMA family and fourth-stage synthesis · Four-unit source package · Eight-component assessment

Two scientific layers

This reader covers 76 historical public Multi-Agent Systems rows, frozen as EMAS-R1. The later accepted AMA-L2 subject also selects 76 identities; its subject SHA-256 is 791620825f2a382e291f05befea096a5eda481f0b9d7b99dc5f7363f7784c0ed. Its later corrections, accepted independent reviews and unresolved scientific observations belong to that exact subject, not to a relabelled September record. SOURCE_MAP.json pins the separate subjects and receipts. The complete current interpretation is retained privately. AMA-DISCLOSE-AMA-L2 remains PENDING for its detailed public release.

Interaction changes the object being judged

Multi-Agent Systems concerns agents whose information, capabilities, control, incentives, commitments or outcomes affect one another. Several named workers do not by themselves establish a useful collective. EMAS-001's decomposition question asks whether an actual interdependence justifies the additional boundaries and coordination cost. Cooperative teams, strategic exchange, public commitments, distributed optimization, organization, argumentation and adaptive collectives are distinct mechanisms with different premises. Historical intake · Historical ledger

The genealogy is plural. Contract Net connects tasks to bidders and contractors; Partial Global Planning coordinates interacting partial plans; joint-intention and SharedPlans accounts explain cooperative persistence and missing contributions; STEAM combines selected teamwork ideas. Public commitment approaches make obligations assessable among parties whose private mental states cannot be inspected. Economic and social-choice results contribute constraints on particular strategic models. Distributed constraints, argumentation and multi-agent learning address still different questions. Direct documentary transmission, shared ancestry, criticism and analogy are separate relation types in the historical genealogy. Historical coverage

A key predicate distinguishes legal messaging from actual fulfillment. A proposal, award, accepted obligation and completed effect need not be the same event. EMAS-010–012 concern conversation causality, information prerequisites and protocol meaning; EMAS-025/026 concern public commitment and valid transitions; EMAS-069/071 concern the collective effect and its evidential closure. A protocol can permit a completion path while participants indefinitely wait. A public promise can remain live when one participant abandons its local plan. An ordinary direct interaction with a sufficiently clear result can satisfy a bounded dependency without a general negotiation or institution. EMAS-010–016, 025–027, 061, 069, 071

Authority, knowledge and capability remain different facts. A role chart does not grant access to an external resource. A norm can be represented without being enforceable. Preventing a prohibited transition, detecting/sanctioning a completed violation and choosing voluntary compliance have different mechanisms. An equilibrium need not be socially desirable; a truthful-reporting result depends on its model and feasible implementation. EMAS-003–004, 030–042

Collective computation also retains its exact object. Constraint satisfaction is not optimization; a distributed optimizer's message count is not its complete memory or transmission cost; argument acceptance is not premise truth. Centralized training with decentralized execution requires a particular information relationship. A restricted monotonic value factorization does not represent every arbitrary joint payoff. A replicated solver remains subject to its actual fault and knowledge premises and the approximation status of its underlying algorithm. EMAS-043–054, 075

Evidence, criticism and failure

Many agents repeating one unverified input do not supply independent observations. Self-play success need not transfer to new partners. Agreement can reflect shared bias, and an individually improved policy can worsen a collective resource outcome. Teamwork and explanation can impose costs without demonstrated benefits under a given task and measurement design. The historical corpus retains positive, negative and null evidence with their source-specific limits. Its 56 bibliography records are not 56 independent experiments. EMAS-006, 048–050, 055–058, 064–067 · Source table

The original EMAS-013 current-FIPA question is unresolved. Historical standards-family records and legal protocol models do not establish a selected runtime's current conformance or interoperability. A publication, deployment or repeated result likewise does not automatically establish comparative causal benefit. A later accepted scientific correction must remain attached to its own source version; the historical rows below are transcribed as history. EMAS-013 and historical intake

Guarded composition and alternatives

The public predecessor offers six configuration branches: direct coordination, revisable cooperative teamwork, strategic public interaction, distributed optimization, controlled adaptive collectives, and a human/institutional overlay. A centralized or direct comparator is admissible when it actually has the required information and powers. It cannot be given powers absent from the real setting merely to make it seem cheaper. The branches remain alternatives, not a mandatory all-of architecture. Historical composition

Four inherited derived rows sharpen the composition: EMAS-071 connects observation, obligation, action and consequence; EMAS-072 governs handoff between assumption regimes; EMAS-073 selects minimum adequate coordination; EMAS-074 checks dependency effects of adaptation. These are analytical criteria with explicit parents and limits. They do not prove the net benefit of a complete deployed collective. More messaging, more members or more formalism is not the acceptance test. EMAS-071–074

Separate native responsibility

Published, canonically unallocated R0044 / #231 preserves the earlier complete 76-member documentary disposition and bounded comparison, including conditional, rejected, research-only and nontriggered outcomes. Its total-cost comparison is inconclusive where the public record says so; this package does not promote that result to broad MAS effectiveness. Existing native qualification

The AMA-L2 rough responsibility asks how a particular current inter-agent dependency is enacted, who remains responsible and which evidence actually reaches the parent or final consumer. R0036/#210 owns the scoped lane/normalization/parent-custody contract; R0035/#209 owns the required joins and single writer; R0033/#207 and R0037/#211 retain governor and lifecycle boundaries. Any later bounded work order belongs under the real existing owner after current integration and approval gates. Owner map · Four rough responsibilities

EMAS-001 — Interdependence-justified agent decomposition (RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM)
Historical origin
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S001; EMAS-S004; EMAS-S045; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S001: Intelligent Agents: Theory and Practice (1995-06); EMAS-S004: The Contract Net Protocol: High-Level Communication and Control in a Distributed Problem Solver (1980-12); EMAS-S045: An Implementation of the Contract Net Protocol Based on Marginal Cost Calculations (1993); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.
Original form
The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.
Development
From loosely coupled distributed problem solving to cost-aware granularity and conditional multi-agent use.
Evolved form at historical freeze
Choose decision boundaries around genuinely separate capabilities, information, incentives or control; compare candidate decompositions against the same collective work.
Problem
Distributed ownership, unavailable central information or genuine concurrent capability can change achievable outcomes.
Mechanism
Choose decision boundaries around genuinely separate capabilities, information, incentives or control; compare candidate decompositions against the same collective work.
Trigger and context
Distributed ownership, unavailable central information or genuine concurrent capability can change achievable outcomes.
Preconditions
A defined task, explicit rights and comparable information/cost accounting.
Decision or consumer
System designer and service recipient
Authority boundary
Architects propose boundaries; operational principals approve rights and resource allocation.
Communication or interaction
Specify which dependencies require messages and whether a fixed direct interface suffices; natural language is optional. Specific coupling: Choose decision boundaries around genuinely separate capabilities, information, incentives or control; compare candidate decompositions against the same collective work.
Observable criterion
A stated dependency is addressed without a worse justified trade-off than the selected comparator.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path
One agent, direct authorised allocation or ordinary services when they meet the same requirements at lower total cost.
Historical disposition
RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM

EMAS-005 — Decentralisation inherently guarantees resilience (REJECTED_OR_DISFAVOURED)
Historical origin
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S001; EMAS-S004; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S001: Intelligent Agents: Theory and Practice (1995-06); EMAS-S004: The Contract Net Protocol: High-Level Communication and Control in a Distributed Problem Solver (1980-12); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.
Original form
The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.
Development
Distribution is retained as a design option, while resilience claims are narrowed to demonstrable mechanisms.
Evolved form at historical freeze
No such unconditional mechanism is retained; replace the claim with a threat-specific account of dependencies, replication and recovery.
Problem
The slogan is often invoked when a system has multiple processes or role names.
Mechanism
No such unconditional mechanism is retained; replace the claim with a threat-specific account of dependencies, replication and recovery.
Trigger and context
The slogan is often invoked when a system has multiple processes or role names.
Preconditions
The rejected claim would require independent failures and suitable redundancy, neither supplied by decentralisation.
Decision or consumer
Reliability reviewer
Authority boundary

EMAS-023 — Joint-goal persistence with explicit reconsideration (RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM)
Historical origin
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S007; EMAS-S011; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S007: On Acting Together (1990); EMAS-S011: Commitments and conventions: The foundation of coordination in multi-agent systems (1993); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.
Original form
Maintain a joint undertaking until recognised achievement, impossibility or irrelevance triggers justified reconsideration and notification.
Development
Logical persistence is coupled to conventions, selective communication and operational repair.
Evolved form at historical freeze
Maintain a joint undertaking until recognised achievement, impossibility or irrelevance triggers justified reconsideration and notification.
Problem
Individual withdrawal would leave other participants relying on a continuing team commitment.
Mechanism
Maintain a joint undertaking until recognised achievement, impossibility or irrelevance triggers justified reconsideration and notification.
Trigger and context
Individual withdrawal would leave other participants relying on a continuing team commitment.
Preconditions
Joint objective, participant set, persistence conditions and a viable notification path.
Decision or consumer
Team members
Authority boundary
A participant may recognise changed conditions but must respect the undertaking’s termination rules.
Communication or interaction
Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Maintain a joint undertaking until recognised achievement, impossibility or irrelevance triggers justified reconsideration and notification.
Observable criterion
An agent does not silently cease contributing while others still reasonably rely on the joint undertaking.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path
Independent work needs no joint-intention machinery; short atomic cooperation may need only an explicit completion/abort contract.
Historical disposition
RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM
Failure modes
Stubborn persistence under harmful goals; cost-prohibitive attempts to establish ideal mutual belief.
Criticism

Contrary evidence

Assumption sensitivity
MATERIAL_ASSUMPTIONS: Joint objective, participant set, persistence conditions and a viable notification path.; CHANGE_THAT_REOPENS_JUDGEMENT: What constitutes sufficient evidence of impossibility or irrelevance?
Unresolved questions

Property versus ceremony
MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_FORM: Independent work needs no joint-intention machinery; short atomic cooperation may need only an explicit completion/abort contract.; ACTUAL_CONSUMER: Team members; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT: Omit or retire when the stated trigger/dependency is absent and the observable postcondition is already secured by a cheaper adequate arrangement; preserve outstanding obligations during any transition.; FALSE_SUBSTITUTE: A representation or label without the stated postcondition and consumer does not establish this candidate.

Original form

Development
Partial and full SharedPlans remain distinct; added structure is not a claim of omniscience.
Evolved form at historical freeze
Relate individual and subgroup plans, recipe knowledge and intentions towards others’ ability to perform their contributions; allow partial elaboration.
Problem
A joint activity needs coordinated subactivities whose details cannot all be fixed initially.
Mechanism
Relate individual and subgroup plans, recipe knowledge and intentions towards others’ ability to perform their contributions; allow partial elaboration.
Trigger and context
A joint activity needs coordinated subactivities whose details cannot all be fixed initially.
Preconditions

Decision or consumer
Team planner
Authority boundary

Communication or interaction
Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Relate individual and subgroup plans, recipe knowledge and intentions towards others’ ability to perform their contributions; allow partial elaboration.
Observable criterion
Unknown plan components remain explicitly partial rather than being mistaken for a completed executable plan.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path
An agreed simple task split or direct request where the richer intentional model adds no decision value.

EMAS-025 — Public conditional commitments (RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM)
Historical origin
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S013; EMAS-S044; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S013: Agent Communication Languages: Rethinking the Principles (1998-12); EMAS-S044: Multiagent Commitment Alignment (2009); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.
Original form
Represent who owes what to whom under which antecedent, and define create, detach, discharge, release and cancellation effects publicly.
Development
Social commitment semantics replaces opaque sincerity as the public accountability object, not as a universal theory of cognition.
Evolved form at historical freeze
Represent who owes what to whom under which antecedent, and define create, detach, discharge, release and cancellation effects publicly.
Problem
Open parties must reason about obligations without inspecting one another’s implementation.
Mechanism
Represent who owes what to whom under which antecedent, and define create, detach, discharge, release and cancellation effects publicly.
Trigger and context
Open parties must reason about obligations without inspecting one another’s implementation.
Preconditions
Creditor/debtor identity, agreed antecedent/consequent meanings and observable lifecycle events.
Decision or consumer
Counterparties and accountability observer
Authority boundary
Debtor, creditor and authorised third parties have different lifecycle powers.
Communication or interaction
Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Represent who owes what to whom under which antecedent, and define create, detach, discharge, release and cancellation effects publicly.
Observable criterion
Participants can distinguish a conditional obligation from an activated or fulfilled one.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path
A direct observable exchange or ordinary contract state when the conditional formalism adds no clarity.

Trigger and context

Preconditions
Reliable ordered messaging and integrity rules for the cited theorem, or a separately analysed replacement transport model.
Decision or consumer
Creditor, debtor and delegate
Authority boundary

Specific coupling: Ensure parties receive the information required to maintain compatible commitment views; delegation changes responsibilities only through specified party-aware operations.
Observable criterion

Development

Evolved form at historical freeze
Observe consequential progress and changed assumptions, notify dependent teammates, and choose repair, reassignment or justified termination.
Problem

Mechanism
Observe consequential progress and changed assumptions, notify dependent teammates, and choose repair, reassignment or justified termination.
Trigger and context

Preconditions

Authority boundary

Specific coupling: Observe consequential progress and changed assumptions, notify dependent teammates, and choose repair, reassignment or justified termination.
Observable criterion
Outstanding obligations are either restored, explicitly changed or visibly left unresolved.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path

EMAS-028 — Selective decision-theoretic communication (CONTEXT_DEPENDENT)
Historical origin
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S009; EMAS-S010; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S009: Towards Flexible Teamwork (1997); EMAS-S010: Team Coordination among Distributed Agents: Analyzing Key Teamwork Theories and Models (2002); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.
Original form
Communicate when expected coordination value outweighs communication and interruption costs, while honouring mandatory notification obligations.
Development
Teamwork theory is narrowed by explicit communication costs rather than maximal belief-sharing as default.
Evolved form at historical freeze
Communicate when expected coordination value outweighs communication and interruption costs, while honouring mandatory notification obligations.
Problem
Communication is costly, distracting or constrained and recipients need different information.
Mechanism
Communicate when expected coordination value outweighs communication and interruption costs, while honouring mandatory notification obligations.
Trigger and context
Communication is costly, distracting or constrained and recipients need different information.
Preconditions
A defensible model of information value and cost, plus non-negotiable safety/commitment notices.
Decision or consumer
Communicating team member
Authority boundary

Communication or interaction
Team communication can notify achievement, impossibility, irrelevance, delegation or repair; some changes require recipients rather than broadcast. Specific coupling: Communicate when expected coordination value outweighs communication and interruption costs, while honouring mandatory notification obligations.
Observable criterion
The chosen policy reduces unnecessary exchange without withholding information required for coordination.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path
Always send a cheap critical notification; use a fixed minimal protocol when estimating value costs more than it saves.

CONTEXT_DEPENDENT
Failure modes

EMAS-046 — Argument acceptability relative to an explicit semantics (CONTEXT_DEPENDENT)
Historical origin
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S031; EMAS-S055; ACCOUNT: Dung (1995) supplies the abstract argumentation antecedent. Amgoud, Maudet and Parsons (2000), §2, explicitly identify that inspiration and add preferences; §§3–5 connect argument reasoning to inter-agent dialogue.; ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: A documented import into a specific MAS dialogue model, not an inferred Dung-to-foundations-book genealogy and not a claim that all MAS argumentation shares the same semantics.
Original form
Construct an argument/attack framework and select a semantics appropriate to the decision; preserve absent or multiple extensions rather than inventing unanimity.
Development
A 1995 abstract reasoning model is explicitly adapted in 2000 to preference-sensitive two-party dialogue; 2002 methodological criticism separates public protocol conformance, private rationality and formal termination. The useful response is a conditional coupling of criterion and operating protocol, not the claim that every dialogue becomes truthful or terminating.
Evolved form at historical freeze
Construct an argument/attack framework and choose its acceptability semantics. Where agents must exchange reasons, couple that criterion to an explicit dialogue with assertion, challenge, support, acceptance and state-update rules appropriate to the selected model; do not treat permissible moves as a termination or truth guarantee.
Problem
Conflicting reasons must be compared without collapsing disagreement to a vote count.
Mechanism
Construct an argument/attack framework and choose its acceptability semantics. Where agents must exchange reasons, couple that criterion to an explicit dialogue with assertion, challenge, support, acceptance and state-update rules appropriate to the selected model; do not treat permissible moves as a termination or truth guarantee.
Trigger and context
Conflicting reasons must be compared without collapsing disagreement to a vote count.
Preconditions
Justified arguments/attacks and a stated grounded, preferred, stable or other semantics.; For the inspected 2000 dialogue branch: two participants, stratified knowledge, static agreed preference ordering, private argument-generation rules and public dialogue stores; relevance and useful stopping require additional behavioural conditions. These are not universal prerequisites of abstract argumentation.
Decision or consumer
Deliberative group and decision consumer
Authority boundary
Decision makers retain responsibility for enacting the selected conclusion.
Communication or interaction
An acceptability criterion needs a consumer. If reasons are communicated, distinguish argument support, challenge, acceptance, public store changes and private rationality assumptions. A dialogical commitment to a proposition does not by itself award a task or create an externally enforceable promise.
Observable criterion
The result is acceptable under the selected framework; no factual truth is inferred from acceptance alone.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path
Direct evidence checking or a simple explicit dispute may suffice when no structured attack analysis is needed.
Historical disposition
CONTEXT_DEPENDENT
Failure modes
Missing or mistaken premises/attacks and absent or multiple extensions can undermine a naive decision rule.; In the inspected dialogue model, legal repeated acceptances or challenges can continue indefinitely; the model assumes relevance and stopping behaviour rather than guaranteeing these from message syntax.; Opaque agents can satisfy observable dialogue rules without establishing the stipulated private reasoning or factual truth.
Criticism

EMAS-074 — Dependency-coupled adaptation (RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM)
Historical origin
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S032; EMAS-S034; EMAS-S050; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S032: Multi-Agent Actor-Critic for Mixed Cooperative–Competitive Environments (2017); EMAS-S034: “Other-Play” for Zero-Shot Coordination (2020); EMAS-S050: Using the Moise+ for a Cooperative Framework of MAS Reorganisation (2004); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.
Original form
The underlying source formulations and their limits are identified by HISTORICAL_ORIGIN; this candidate is an analytical extraction, not an attributed quotation or historical doctrine.
Development
Analytical link between non-stationary learning and organisational/commitment continuity, not autonomous self-authorisation.
Evolved form at historical freeze
Evaluate a changed policy, protocol or organisation against the dependent commitments, resource constraints and partner expectations before treating local improvement as an accepted collective revision.
Problem
Learning or structural change can alter another actor’s relied-upon behaviour.
Mechanism
Evaluate a changed policy, protocol or organisation against the dependent commitments, resource constraints and partner expectations before treating local improvement as an accepted collective revision.
Trigger and context
Learning or structural change can alter another actor’s relied-upon behaviour.
Preconditions
Declared dependency boundary, relevant collective tests and a way to preserve or renegotiate in-flight expectations.
Decision or consumer
Learning controller and affected counterparty
Authority boundary
Learning and organisational change remain bounded by externally established rights.
Communication or interaction
Communication couples local evidence to relevant consumers and actions; successful reporting is not successful operation. Specific coupling: Evaluate a changed policy, protocol or organisation against the dependent commitments, resource constraints and partner expectations before treating local improvement as an accepted collective revision.
Observable criterion
Adaptation preserves required interfaces or changes them explicitly; improved local score alone is insufficient.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path
Local change proceeds without broad review when its effects are demonstrably contained within unchanged interfaces.
Historical disposition
RETAINED_IN_EVOLVED_FORM
Failure modes
Continuous mutual adaptation invalidates the test population before results can guide action.
Criticism
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S035; EMAS-S040; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S035; LIMITS: Eleven algorithms, five seeds, 100 test episodes per checkpoint; reported selected-policy returns and 75% intervals. Limited tuning and selection convention constrain comparisons; no strategic open-world validation.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S040; LIMITS: Two-player discretised pricing simulation. Tabular pretraining: 500 games/1,000 policies; LLM comparison selects six GPT5-mini meta-strategies, 40 base-game samples, 50 rounds, four prices. Known cost types in meta-analysis and restricted strategy set limit transfer.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Continuous mutual adaptation invalidates the test population before results can guide action.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.
Contrary evidence

Assumption sensitivity

Property versus ceremony
MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_FORM: Local change proceeds without broad review when its effects are demonstrably contained within unchanged interfaces.; ACTUAL_CONSUMER: Learning controller and affected counterparty; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT: Omit or retire when the stated trigger/dependency is absent and the observable postcondition is already secured by a cheaper adequate arrangement; preserve outstanding obligations during any transition.; FALSE_SUBSTITUTE: A representation or label without the stated postcondition and consumer does not establish this candidate.

EMAS-075 — Fault-model-specific replicated optimisation (DOMAIN_SPECIFIC)
Historical origin
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S051; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S051: Byzantine Fault Tolerance in Distributed Constraint Optimization Problems (2026-05); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.
Original form
Replicate the required factor/variable computations and verify sequence-correlated deterministic messages under the FT-DCOP replica assumptions, preserving fault-free Max-Sum behaviour.
Development
2026 FT-DCOP explicitly extends the model; it is not a universal Byzantine recipe or an independence theorem.
Evolved form at historical freeze
Replicate the required factor/variable computations and verify sequence-correlated deterministic messages under the FT-DCOP replica assumptions, preserving fault-free Max-Sum behaviour.
Problem
Arbitrary faulty DCOP participants are in scope and the required knowledge/replica coverage is feasible.
Mechanism
Replicate the required factor/variable computations and verify sequence-correlated deterministic messages under the FT-DCOP replica assumptions, preserving fault-free Max-Sum behaviour.
Trigger and context
Arbitrary faulty DCOP participants are in scope and the required knowledge/replica coverage is feasible.
Preconditions
For the analysed construction, at least 2k+1 replicas per relevant group, at most k faulty, correct function knowledge and the specified synchronous message-passing and round discipline.
Decision or consumer
DCOP reliability designer
Authority boundary
Replicated computations do not authorise actions outside the optimisation participants’ rights.
Communication or interaction
Failure notices, state reconciliation and retry messages require correlation; network partitions do not create new authority. Specific coupling: Replicate the required factor/variable computations and verify sequence-correlated deterministic messages under the FT-DCOP replica assumptions, preserving fault-free Max-Sum behaviour.
Observable criterion
Correct replicas reproduce the fault-free underlying algorithm’s messages under the model; approximation quality is not strengthened into exact optimality.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path
Crash recovery or unreplicated optimisation when Byzantine behaviour is out of scope and lower-cost controls suffice.
Historical disposition
DOMAIN_SPECIFIC
Failure modes
Common corrupt utilities, too many faulty replicas or incompatible transport defeat the guarantee; communication overhead grows quadratically with k per original message.
Criticism
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S051; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S051; LIMITS: At most k faulty replicas per relevant set and at least 2k+1 knowledge/replica coverage are material; communication multiplication is O(k²) per original message. Main paper inspected; supplementary impossibility proof not independently checked. Synthetic experiments do not certify arbitrary Byzantine MAS.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Common corrupt utilities, too many faulty replicas or incompatible transport defeat the guarantee; communication overhead grows quadratically with k per original message.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.
Contrary evidence
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S051; ADJUDICATION: Common corrupt utilities, too many faulty replicas or incompatible transport defeat the guarantee; communication overhead grows quadratically with k per original message.; NOT_A_REFUTATION_OF: A bounded theorem whose actual assumptions remain satisfied.
Assumption sensitivity
MATERIAL_ASSUMPTIONS: For the analysed construction, at least 2k+1 replicas per relevant group, at most k faulty, correct function knowledge and the specified synchronous message-passing and round discipline.; CHANGE_THAT_REOPENS_JUDGEMENT: Can the deployment actually supply independently correct utility knowledge and the required replica distribution?
Unresolved questions
Can the deployment actually supply independently correct utility knowledge and the required replica distribution?
Property versus ceremony
MINIMAL_ADEQUATE_FORM: Crash recovery or unreplicated optimisation when Byzantine behaviour is out of scope and lower-cost controls suffice.; ACTUAL_CONSUMER: DCOP reliability designer; OMISSION_OR_RETIREMENT: Omit or retire when the stated trigger/dependency is absent and the observable postcondition is already secured by a cheaper adequate arrangement; preserve outstanding obligations during any transition.; FALSE_SUBSTITUTE: A representation or label without the stated postcondition and consumer does not establish this candidate.

EMAS-076 — Norm-aware plan augmentation and deliberation (DOMAIN_SPECIFIC)
Historical origin
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S052; ACCOUNT: EMAS-S052: Engineering Norm-aware BDI Agents (2026-05); ATTRIBUTION_LIMIT: These are inspected antecedents, not a claim to have identified every earliest precursor.
Original form
Translate applicable norms into goal-plan choices that distinguish added compliance steps, violation consequences and non-applicability, then integrate them with the agent’s plan selection.
Development
2026 norm-aware BDI work demonstrates a running-example implementation in an existing platform; general overhead and dynamic revision remain research questions.
Evolved form at historical freeze
Translate applicable norms into goal-plan choices that distinguish added compliance steps, violation consequences and non-applicability, then integrate them with the agent’s plan selection.
Problem
An agent must reason about a norm rather than merely have forbidden actions blocked externally.
Mechanism
Translate applicable norms into goal-plan choices that distinguish added compliance steps, violation consequences and non-applicability, then integrate them with the agent’s plan selection.
Trigger and context
An agent must reason about a norm rather than merely have forbidden actions blocked externally.
Preconditions
Known norm conditions, available compliance actions and a justified valuation/plan-selection procedure.
Decision or consumer
Agent programmer and normative interaction designer
Authority boundary
Ability to represent violation is descriptive decision support, not permission to violate real obligations.
Communication or interaction
Role adoption, mission acceptance, normative changes and violations have identifiable communicative or environmental evidence. Specific coupling: Translate applicable norms into goal-plan choices that distinguish added compliance steps, violation consequences and non-applicability, then integrate them with the agent’s plan selection.
Observable criterion
The selected plan reflects norm-related steps and consequences in the implementation’s represented choices.
Nontrigger or cheaper adequate path
A fixed compliant plan or direct enforcement when deliberating about alternatives adds no necessary flexibility.
Historical disposition
DOMAIN_SPECIFIC
Failure modes
Incorrect valuations normalise harmful violations; norm awareness is mistaken for compliance or legitimacy.
Criticism
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S020; EMAS-S021; SOURCE_LIMITS: SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S020; LIMITS: The model assumes actions observable and effects determined by the norm artefact. A simulated fine does not establish legal power over real people. Criticism of other frameworks is scoped to versions discussed.; SOURCE_ID: EMAS-S021; LIMITS: Constructed regulated water-market scenario, not evidence of real-world judicial enforcement. The 2010 event and 2011 publication dates are distinct. PDF visual-rendering attempt failed; text sections were readable.; RESEARCHER_OBJECTION: Incorrect valuations normalise harmful violations; norm awareness is mistaken for compliance or legitimacy.; ATTRIBUTION: Source criticism and this candidate-level analytical objection are distinct; the latter is not attributed as a quotation.
Contrary evidence
SOURCE_IDS: EMAS-S020; EMAS-S021; ADJUDICATION: Incorrect valuations normalise harmful violations; norm awareness is mistaken for compliance or legitimacy.; NOT_A_REFUTATION_OF: A bounded theorem whose actual assumptions remain satisfied.
Assumption sensitivity
MATERIAL_ASSUMPTIONS: Known norm conditions, available compliance actions and a justified valuation/plan-selection procedure.; CHANGE_THAT_REOPENS_JUDGEMENT: How are conflicting or newly introduced norms handled beyond the running example?
Unresolved questions
